WQX Web maps import columns **by position** (`ColumnNumber`), not by header name (consistent with \"Ignore First Row\" being checked). The only saved Results & Activities configuration for this organization, `KWF_Results_Baseline_Template` (Uid 8515, description: \"intended to mimic the AQWMS format provided by ADEC in Spring 2022\"), is an old 44-column template with a completely different column order. Its position 9 expects a numeric `Activity Latitude`; our export puts `Activity End Date` (a date string) there -- guaranteed to crash parsing on the very first data row, exactly matching the observed \"Row 2\" failure (row 2 of the file = first data row, after the header). <div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01NvQCRfcZeW8kQc7ZXp8egb"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"diff --git a/AGENTS.md b/AGENTS.md\nindex 614661d..720479c 100644\n--- a/AGENTS.md\n+++ b/AGE...[truncated, total 8567 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"diff --git a/AGENTS.md b/AGENTS.md\nindex 614661d..720479c 100644\n--- a/AGENTS.md\n+++ b/AGENTS.md\n@@ -150,6 +150,19 @@ Analyte name → abbreviation used in WQX Activity IDs (27 rows as of April 2026\n | 10000424 | RM 79.5 – Juneau Creek         |\n | 10000425 | RM 82 – Kenai Lake Bridge      |\n \n+### ID rule: never include the `KENAI_WQX-` prefix in any ID column\n+\n+WQX Web prepends `KENAI_WQX-` to IDs on import. WQP downloads already include it, so a value copied from WQP must have the prefix stripped. Upload files use bare IDs: `Monitoring Location ID` = `10000002`, station `MonitoringLocationIdentifier` = `10000002`, Activity IDs built from site code, date, and analyte (no prefix).\n+\n+On 2026-10-14 the 2021 files were uploaded with prefixed location IDs. WQP then held 22 duplicate stations (`KENAI_WQX-KENAI_WQX-100000xx`) carrying all 830 results; cleanup (delete files, WQX Web delete imports, re-upload) is documented in the report repo at `other/output/epa_wqp_uploads/2026-10-20_doubled_station_cleanup/README.md`.\n+\n+`other/misc/qaqc_repo_transition/functions/generate_cdx_export.R` now builds bare IDs and stops with an error before writing if any ID column starts with `KENAI_WQX`, if a location ID is not one of the 22 known IDs above, or if `station.csv` is not exactly those 22 stations. Notes for future work:\n+\n+- In the `adec_site_names` sheet of `wqx_template_matching_table.xlsx`, the column `MonitoringLocationIdentifier` holds the site code (`KBL_*`), not a WQP identifier. The script renames it to `site_code`.\n+- That sheet previously listed `KBL_t_50.0` and `KBL_t_70.0` for IDs 10000030 and 10000031. Both are mainstem sites (`m`), matching the WQP station names `KBL_m_50.0` and `KBL_m_70.0`. Corrected to `m` on 2026-10-20 (same fix in the report repo copy). The 2021 Activity IDs for those two sites (34 rows) previously used `t`; they were deleted from WQP on 2026-10-20 and the re-upload uses `m`.\n+- This script copy is older than the report repo's `functions/appendix_a_scripts/generate_cdx_export.R` (for example, it still maps `Biological Intent` and `Subject Taxonomic Name`, which were removed on 2026-10-14). Port the remaining fixes before using it for upload.\n+- 2025 output currently has 116 rows with duplicated Activity IDs (same characteristic, fraction, and method). Not introduced by the ID change; investigate before upload.\n+\n ------------------------------------------------------------------------\n \n ## 2025.qmd Pipeline Structure\ndiff --git a/other/misc/qaqc_repo_transition/functions/generate_cdx_export.R b/other/misc/qaqc_repo_transition/functions/generate_cdx_export.R\nindex 29bd614..1a5b0f5 100644\n--- a/other/misc/qaqc_repo_transition/functions/generate_cdx_export.R\n+++ b/other/misc/qaqc_repo_transition/functions/generate_cdx_export.R\n@@ -31,6 +31,25 @@\n #   other/output/results_activities.csv\n #   other/output/project.csv\n #   other/output/station.csv\n+#\n+# ID RULE (learned 2026-10-14/20): never put the \"KENAI_WQX-\" org prefix in any\n+# ID column (Monitoring Location ID, MonitoringLocationIdentifier, Activity ID).\n+# WQX Web prepends the prefix itself on import. A prefixed ID is stored as\n+# \"KENAI_WQX-KENAI_WQX-...\" and creates duplicate stations that hold the results.\n+# Checks near each write below stop the script before a bad file is written.\n+# Details: other/output/epa_wqp_uploads/2026-10-20_doubled_station_cleanup/README.md\n+# in the kenai-river-wqx report repo.\n+\n+# Stops with an error if any value starts with the org prefix\n+assert_no_org_prefix \u003c- function(x, label) {\n+  bad \u003c- !is.na(x) & grepl(\"^KENAI_WQX\", x, ignore.case = TRUE)\n+  if (any(bad)) {\n+    stop(sum(bad), \" value(s) in \", label, \" start with the org prefix 'KENAI_WQX'. \",\n+         \"WQX Web adds the prefix on import; strip it before upload. First offender: \",\n+         x[bad][1], call. = FALSE)\n+  }\n+  invisible(TRUE)\n+}\n \n \n # ── Results and Activities ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n@@ -50,12 +69,29 @@ export_dat %\u003c>%\n export_dat %\u003c>%\n   filter(!(characteristic_name == \"Phosphorus\" & result_analytical_method_id == \"200.8\"))\n \n-# Re-join WQP site location info (site names) needed for Activity ID construction\n+# Re-join site codes needed for Activity ID construction.\n+# NOTE: in this sheet the column \"MonitoringLocationIdentifier\" holds the site\n+# CODE (e.g. KBL_m_01.5), not a WQP identifier, and \"MonitoringLocationName\" is a\n+# descriptive name. Rename to avoid confusion and join explicitly on the numeric ID.\n site_name_match \u003c- read_excel(\n   file.path(cfg$templates_dir, \"wqx_template_matching_table.xlsx\"),\n   sheet = \"adec_site_names\"\n-) %>% remove_empty()\n-export_dat \u003c- left_join(export_dat, site_name_match)\n+) %>%\n+  remove_empty() %>%\n+  transmute(monitoring_location_id = as.character(monitoring_location_id),\n+            site_code = MonitoringLocationIdentifier)\n+\n+# The 22 valid bare location IDs, used by the checks below\n+valid_location_ids \u003c- site_name_match$monitoring_location_id\n+\n+export_dat %\u003c>% mutate(monitoring_location_id = as.character(monitoring_location_id))\n+export_dat \u003c- left_join(export_dat, site_name_match, by = \"monitoring_location_id\")\n+\n+if (any(is.na(export_dat$site_code))) {\n+  stop(\"Some rows have no site code in the 'adec_site_names' lookup: \",\n+       paste(unique(export_dat$monitoring_location_id[is.na(export_dat$site_code)]),\n+             collapse = \", \"), call. = FALSE)\n+}\n \n # Re-join analyte abbreviations for Activity ID construction\n analyte_abbrev \u003c- read.csv(file.path(cfg$templates_dir, \"analytes_list_manual_edit.csv\"))\n@@ -74,14 +110,15 @@ export_dat %\u003c>%\n export_dat %\u003c>%\n   mutate(\n     `Project ID`                           = 10000007,\n-    `Monitoring Location ID`               = MonitoringLocationIdentifier,\n+    # Bare numeric ID (no org prefix); WQX Web prepends \"KENAI_WQX-\" on import\n+    `Monitoring Location ID`               = monitoring_location_id,\n     `Activity Media Name`                  = activity_media_name,\n     `Activity Media Subdivision Name`      = activity_media_subdivision_name,\n     `Activity ID` = case_when(\n       is.na(sample_condition_abbrv) ~\n-        paste0(`MonitoringLocationName`, \"-\", activity_start_date, \"-\", analyte_abbreviation),\n+        paste0(site_code, \"-\", activity_start_date, \"-\", analyte_abbreviation),\n       !is.na(sample_condition_abbrv) ~\n-        paste0(`MonitoringLocationName`, \"-\", activity_start_date, \"-\",\n+        paste0(site_code, \"-\", activity_start_date, \"-\",\n                analyte_abbreviation, \"-\", sample_condition_abbrv)\n     ),\n     `Activity Start Date`                  = activity_start_date,\n@@ -143,6 +180,16 @@ wqx_colnames \u003c- read_excel(cfg$wqx_template_file,\n   colnames()\n export_dat %\u003c>% select(one_of(wqx_colnames))\n \n+# Checks before writing: ID form\n+assert_no_org_prefix(export_dat$`Monitoring Location ID`, \"Monitoring Location ID\")\n+assert_no_org_prefix(export_dat$`Activity ID`, \"Activity ID\")\n+stopifnot(\n+  \"Monitoring Location ID must be one of the 22 known bare IDs\" =\n+    all(as.character(export_dat$`Monitoring Location ID`) %in% valid_location_ids),\n+  \"Activity ID must not be NA\" = !anyNA(export_dat$`Activity ID`),\n+  \"Activity ID must be 55 characters or fewer\" = all(nchar(export_dat$`Activity ID`) \u003c= 55)\n+)\n+\n # Write final results and activities CSV for CDX upload\n write.csv(export_dat, here::here(\"other/output/results_activities.csv\"), row.names = FALSE)\n \n@@ -186,6 +233,8 @@ station_upload \u003c- read.csv(\n   file.path(cfg$wqx_downloads_dir, \"station/station.csv\")\n ) |>\n   dplyr::filter(grepl(\"KBL\", MonitoringLocationName)) |>\n+  # WQP IDs carry the \"KENAI_WQX-\" prefix; WQX Web adds it back on import\n+  dplyr::mutate(MonitoringLocationIdentifier = sub(\"^KENAI_WQX-\", \"\", MonitoringLocationIdentifier)) |>\n   dplyr::select(\n     MonitoringLocationIdentifier,\n     MonitoringLocationName,\n@@ -207,4 +256,13 @@ station_upload \u003c- read.csv(\n     TRUE ~ HorizontalCollectionMethodName\n   ))\n \n+# Checks before writing: ID form. A leftover prefix here usually means the WQP\n+# download still contains the doubled \"KENAI_WQX-KENAI_WQX-...\" stations.\n+assert_no_org_prefix(station_upload$MonitoringLocationIdentifier, \"station MonitoringLocationIdentifier\")\n+stopifnot(\n+  \"station.csv must contain exactly the 22 known station IDs, once each\" =\n+    setequal(as.character(station_upload$MonitoringLocationIdentifier), valid_location_ids) &&\n+    !anyDuplicated(station_upload$MonitoringLocationIdentifier)\n+)\n+\n write.csv(station_upload, here::here(\"other/output/station.csv\"), row.names = FALSE)\n8","language":"text"},"elapsedMs":91}</script></tool-result></div>

`quarto render` was failing because of the project config, not the template. Your local commit `386a8ae` changed `_quarto.yml` from a book (with an explicit chapter list) to `type: default`. A default project renders every `.qmd` and `.md` file it finds. That includes `other/misc/qaqc_repo_transition/templates/pipeline_template.qmd`, whose `year-config` chunk still has the placeholder `XXXX`, so it stopped there. It would also have picked up `session_log.md`, `reference/dat_contract.md`, the old `qa_qc_template.qmd`, and the legacy 2014–2024 chapters.

I added a `render:` list to the `project:` block of [_quarto.yml](_quarto.yml) so only `project_year/2025.qmd` is rendered. `quarto inspect` now lists just that file. I haven't run the full render, so `2025.qmd` itself is untested. Part D is guarded, so it should skip cleanly if the WQP downloads aren't there.

If you meant to keep the book layout, restore the `book:` chapter list instead. I assumed the switch to `type: default` was deliberate. To render another year, add it to the `render:` list, or run `quarto render project_year/2023.qmd`.

<div><tool-call id="toolu_0196CN2rwfq3AU7nPymKrDKT"><script type="application/json">{"version":2,"name":"write","input":{"file_path":"D:\\GitHub\\kenai-river-wqx-qaqc\\intro.qmd","content":"# Introduction\n\nThis project prepares one year of Kenai River Baseline Water Quality Monit...[truncated, total 1905 chars]","overwrite":true},"caption":"intro.qmd","captionLink":"intro.qmd","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"# Introduction\n\nThis project prepares one year of Kenai River Baseline Water Quality Monitoring data, collected by the Kenai Watershed Forum, for submission to the U.S. EPA Water Quality Exchange (WQX) through CDX. Once submitted, the data are published through EPA's Water Quality Portal (WQP), where the [report repository](https://github.com/Kenai-Watershed-Forum/kenai-river-wqx) reads them for display and interpretation.\n\n## One year at a time\n\nThe pipeline is a template that is run for a single data year. The working year is set in `config.yml` (sampling dates and raw file paths) and in the year configuration chunk of `project_year/\u003cyear>.qmd`. Raw lab files for that year are placed in `data/`, which is not tracked in git.\n\n## Pipeline steps\n\n1. **Part A: ingestion.** Lab files (SGS metals, nutrients and BTEX; SWWTP fecal coliform and TSS; field YSI and turbidity) are read and combined into one data frame, `dat`. Lab QC rows are written to `other/output/intermediate/lab_qaqc_data/` and excluded.\n2. **Part B: WQX formatting.** `functions/format_wqx.R` joins site coordinates, sample fraction, preservative and container information, and builds Activity IDs.\n3. **Part C: QA/QC checklist.** A 42-question checklist based on ADEC guidance, with data flags applied from `reference/wqx_templates/\u003cyear>_data_flag_decisions.csv`.\n4. **Part D: CDX export.** `functions/generate_cdx_export.R` writes the upload files (`results_activities.csv`, `project.csv`, `station.csv`) to `other/output/`. It requires station and project files downloaded manually from WQP into `other/input/WQX_downloads/`.\n\nLookup tables (site names, analysis codes, analyte abbreviations, holding times) are in `reference/wqx_templates/`.\n\n## ID rule\n\nUpload files must use bare IDs with no `KENAI_WQX-` prefix, because WQX Web adds the prefix on import. The export script stops with an error if a prefixed ID is found.\n","language":"markdown"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01LDbj1c9Bxe4kPnMzH2ga8U"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 11.5KB, 194 lines, showing lines 1-194]\n1\t# QA/QC Repo as a Single-Year Template: R...[truncated, total 12445 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 11.5KB, 194 lines, showing lines 1-194]\n# QA/QC Repo as a Single-Year Template: Revised Plan (rev. 2, 2026-10-28)\n\nStatus: APPROVED 2026-10-28; layout, phases, and decisions rewritten the same day after\nthe decisions below. Phase A in progress (step 1: `reference/dat_contract.md` drafted in\nthe qaqc repo). Supersedes Phase 2 and Phase 3 of\n`2026-10-14-1300-per-year-repo-architecture-migration.md` (Phases 0 and 1 stand).\n\n## Concept\n\n`kenai-river-wqx-qaqc` is a **template repo for one data year at a time**. When a year is\nbeing worked, the repo holds only that year's raw lab files, config, outputs, and\npreliminary report, plus shared code and reference data. A finished year is archived as a\nGitHub Release \"year bundle\" and removed from the working tree. Years are developed one at\na time, starting with 2025 and working backwards (2024, 2023, ...).\n\nBecause intake and output handling need customizing and troubleshooting for each year,\nthe working copy of the pipeline is edited directly for the current year. General fixes are\npromoted back into the template at archive time.\n\n## Decision changes from the 2026-10-14 plan\n\n| Old decision | New decision |\n|---|---|\n| New repo `kenai-river-baseline-wqx-template` | Not created. `kenai-river-wqx-qaqc` is the template. |\n| One repo per data year | One repo, one working year at a time; past years archived as release bundles. |\n| Archive `kenai-river-wqx-qaqc` | Not archived. Active home for QA/QC and the preliminary report. |\n| Preliminary report generalized in a new repo | Same idea, lives in `kenai-river-wqx-qaqc`. |\n\nUnchanged: `kenai-river-wqx` (report repo) is standalone, reads only from WQP, and does not\ndepend on the qaqc repo (only `session_log.md` is synced by Action). Shared reference data\n(`master_reg_limits.xlsx`, `baseline_sites.csv`, WQX matching tables) are single copies in\nthe qaqc repo.\n\n## Target repo layout (`kenai-river-wqx-qaqc`), working year only\n\n```\nqaqc.qmd                  # QA/QC pipeline for the working year (Parts A-D); edited per year\nprelim_report.qmd         # narrative report; reads only assessed_results.csv\nconfig.yml                # working year: dates, input file names, sheet/skip settings\nfunctions/\n  ingest_sgs.R  ingest_fc.R  ingest_tss.R  ingest_ysi.R   # per-lab starting points, emit dat contract\n  format_wqx.R  apply_qaqc_flags.R  generate_cdx_export.R\n  fetch_raw_data.R  archive_year.R                          # restore / bundle a year\n  (report helpers carried from kenai-river-wqx)\nreference/                # master_reg_limits.xlsx, baseline_sites.csv, WQX matching tables, dat_contract.md\ndata/                     # raw lab files for the working year (contents gitignored)\ndata/{year}.sha256        # committed checksum per archived year\noutput/                   # dat, lab_qc_assessed.csv, assessed_results.csv, CDX files, rendered report\nproject_year/             # legacy per-year QMDs, read-only until retired\n```\n\nPast years live only as GitHub Release assets (see decisions 10 to 12). History already in\nthe qaqc repo (~460 MB) is left as is.\n\n## The seam: the `dat` contract\n\nIngestion ends at a standardized `dat` data frame (`reference/dat_contract.md`). Downstream\nsteps use only that contract: WQX formatting, QA/QC flags, the 42-question checklist, CDX\nexport, and the report. The QA/QC stage writes `output/assessed_results.csv`; the report\nreads only that file (schema in decision 9).\n\n## Phases\n\nEach phase ends with a pause for user review. There is no cross-year regression baseline;\n2025 is verified by explaining every difference from the existing 2025 outputs\n(`2025.qmd`, `2025_preliminary_summary.qmd`), including known defects (surrogates in `dat`,\nfield blank coding, `mg/l` case).\n\n### Phase A: Contract, layout, and archive mechanics\n1. Finish `reference/dat_contract.md` (`dat` and `lab_qc_dat`).\n2. Create the layout above (empty scaffolds); gitignore `data/` contents and `output/`\n   as agreed; move shared reference data into `reference/`.\n3. Reconcile the three function copies (`other/misc/qaqc_repo_transition/functions/`)\n   into `functions/`.\n4. Write `fetch_raw_data(year)` (download release zip, verify `data/{year}.sha256`) and\n   `archive_year(year)` (build the year bundle, write checksum), and document the\n   new-year and archive checklists in the README.\n\n### Phase B: 2025 ingestion as functions\n1. Extract SGS, FC, TSS, YSI ingestion from `2025.qmd` into `functions/ingest_*.R`\n   (season argument; 2025 quirks in `config.yml` or edited directly).\n2. Route `(surr)` analytes to `lab_qc_dat`; keep trip blanks in field data; drop `matrix`\n   and `result_detection_condition`; add the `mg/L` guard.\n3. Add a contract-check function that stops with a clear message on any violation.\n4. Verify: `dat` row counts and values vs `2025.qmd`, with each known difference explained.\n\n### Phase C: QA/QC and CDX export\n1. Build `qaqc.qmd` Parts B-D on the contract output; add field blank coding (Activity\n   Type `Quality Control Sample-Field Blank`, suffix `FB`) and the surrogate check in\n   Question 20.\n2. Write `output/assessed_results.csv` and `output/lab_qc_assessed.csv`.\n3. Verify against the 2025 CDX export files and checklist results; explain differences.\n\n### Phase D: Narrative report\n1. Generalize `2025_preliminary_summary.qmd` into `prelim_report.qmd`: delete its\n   ~330-line setup-chunk re-ingestion (about lines 200-530) and read\n   `assessed_results.csv` instead.\n2. Parameterize year, dates, captions, and photo references; stop if the assessed file\n   is missing or stale.\n3. Verify 2025 numbers and tables against the existing summary.\n\n### Phase E: Archive 2025 and set up for 2024\n1. Run the archive checklist: build the 2025 bundle, upload release `raw-data-2025`,\n   commit its checksum file.\n2. Promotion review: diff the 2025 working files against the template and decide what\n   goes back into the template (assistant drafts the diff).\n3. Reset the working tree for 2024; retire the 2025 and 2023 legacy `project_year` files\n   as each year is superseded.\n4. Update both repos' `AGENTS.md` and README files for the single-year template design.\n\nLater years (2024, 2023, ...) repeat: start year, customize ingestion, QA/QC, report,\narchive, promotion review.\n\n## Decisions log (2026-10-28)\n\n1. (Superseded by 8i/decision 11.) Earlier: 2021 as regression baseline. Dropped because\n   per-year customization means one year cannot validate another. 2021 gets no special role;\n   it is reached in the backwards sequence like any year.\n2. Surrogates are ingested as designated lab QC samples (`lab_qc_dat`), not field data.\n3. Field blanks only exist from about late 2022 or 2023 onward; field-blank handling must\n   be optional per year.\n4. `dat` drops `matrix` and `result_detection_condition` (24 columns). `dissolved` stays in\n   both `dat` and `lab_qc_dat`.\n5. Field blank coding: Activity Type `Quality Control Sample-Field Blank`, Activity ID suffix\n   `FB`. Trip blank suffix `Blank` is unchanged. HMW charts exclude Activity Types containing\n   \"quality\" (station summary counts and WQP downloads are unfiltered).\n6. `lab_qc_dat`: all 14 SGS lab QC codes plus `(surr)` analytes from any sample type; keeps\n   recovery/RPD columns and the lab-supplied limits (shipped in the EDD, used by Question\n   20). Question 20 extends to surrogates. Never flows into the WQX export. Profile and\n   proposed columns: `reference/dat_contract.md`.\n7. Lab format: one ingest function per lab (SGS, FC, TSS, YSI) taking a season argument.\n   The `adapters/{year}.R` idea is dropped (see 11).\n8. Narrative report: separate `prelim_report.qmd`, rendered after `qaqc.qmd`, reading only\n   `output/assessed_results.csv`, `config.yml`, and reference data (never raw lab files).\n   Failed data is excluded through flag columns; the report applies no filters of its own.\n9. `assessed_results.csv`: the 24 `dat` columns under ingest names plus `result_id` (stable\n   row key within the year), `qaqc_status` (Accepted/Rejected/Flagged), `qaqc_flags`\n   (semicolon-separated checklist questions, empty string if none), `qaqc_note` (NA if none),\n   and `use_in_report` (derived from `qaqc_status`). One row per result; lab QC rows are\n   excluded and written to `output/lab_qc_assessed.csv`. WQX renaming and Activity IDs stay\n   in the export step. The report and the CDX export both derive accept/reject from\n   `qaqc_status`.\n10. Raw data hosting: the repo holds one year's raw data at a time (`data/` contents\n    gitignored). Archived years are GitHub Releases, one zip per year (`raw-data-{year}`) on\n    the qaqc repo. The repo is public and all lab files (PDFs, COC scans, .mdb, data sheets)\n    may be published, so zips hold everything the labs sent and fetching needs no login. A new\n    year's files are placed by hand in `data/`; an archived year is restored with\n    `fetch_raw_data(year)`. Each archived year has a committed checksum file\n    (`data/{year}.sha256`) verified on fetch.\n11. Year bundle: each release zip holds raw lab files, `config.yml`, the edited pipeline\n    files for that year, `output/` (including QA/QC flags), and the rendered report. The\n    repo holds only the working year. Existing history is left as is (no rewrite). Years are\n    developed one at a time, 2025 first and then backwards; no cross-year regression check.\n    Per-year customization is done by editing the working copy directly (the repo is a\n    template); those edited files are archived in the bundle.\n12. Promoting fixes: the archive checklist includes a review step that diffs the year's\n    working files against the template; you decide what to copy back, and the assistant\n    drafts the diff.\n\n## Open items\n\n- Confirmed 2026-10-28: hard-stop `mg/L` guard at ingest (same `stopifnot()` as the report\n  repo) and the `session_log.md` sync Action stays as is.\n- Checked 2026-10-28: 2025 raw files total 31 MB (largest file 15.6 MB), far under the 2 GB\n  per-asset release limit.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- Changes to how the report repo reads WQP or renders the book.\n- Performing CDX submissions for any year.\n- Rewriting qaqc repo git history.\n13. Quarto setup (2026-10-28): the qaqc repo's Quarto book config is replaced by a plain\n    project (`_quarto.yml`, type default). `qaqc.qmd` and `prelim_report.qmd` render on their\n    own; legacy `project_year/*.qmd` remain read-only files outside the build until retired.\n\n14. Here is the design for `functions/ingest_sgs.R`. I won't write any code until you confirm.

**What it does**

`ingest_sgs(season, cfg)` replaces the two near-identical spring and summer chunks in `2025.qmd` (about lines 101–340). The season argument selects the file and sheet from `config.yml` (`files$sgs$spring` or `$summer`). It returns a list with two data frames:

- **`dat`:** field results, in the contract's 24 columns, for that season.
- **`lab_qc`:** lab QC rows, in the proposed `lab_qc_dat` columns from `reference/dat_contract.md` (recovery, RPD, limits, batch IDs and so on).

The steps, in order:

1. Read the EDD and clean the names.
2. Parse the three date-time strings.
3. Strip the `EP` prefix from the method.
4. Set `result_sample_fraction` from the `dissolved` flag, for EP200.8 only. Nutrients and the rest stay NA for the `format_wqx.R` lookup.
5. Join the analysis-code table for `epa_analysis_id` and `context_code`.
6. Join the SGS site table for `monitoring_location_id` and `sample_condition`.
7. Split the rows. Any row whose `sample_type` is one of the 14 lab QC codes, or whose analyte contains `(surr)`, goes to `lab_qc`. The rest go to `dat`.

**Differences from today's `2025.qmd`** (each will be listed in the verification step)

- The 24 surrogate rows move from `dat` to `lab_qc`.
- `matrix` and `result_detection_condition` are dropped.
- A `mg/L` guard: the function stops if any unit differs only by case from `mg/L`. SGS units for 2025 are already correct, so this shouldn't fire here.
- The function returns data instead of writing `sgs_als_qaqc_dat*.csv`. `qaqc.qmd` will write `lab_qc_dat` itself.
- Unmatched sample IDs or analysis codes stop with a clear message instead of silently becoming NA.
- The `!is.na(result)` filter stays as it is, so row counts remain comparable.

**Check:** `dat` from spring plus summer, after trip blank expansion, should equal today's 567 SGS rows minus the 24 surrogates, which is 543. I'd confirm the values against `2025.qmd` column by column.

**Decision for you:** where should the trip blank site mapping live? Trip blanks labelled "RM1.5&6.5" and "RM40&43" each cover two sites and are currently a hardcoded tribble in the QMD. I suggest putting the mapping in `config.yml` under `sgs: trip_blank_sites`. The function would then expand them with no 2025 values in the code, which fits a blank template better. The alternative is to keep it in the function body as an editable default.


<span class="suggestion">Put the trip blank site mapping in config.yml.</span>


<span class="suggestion">Keep the trip blank site mapping hardcoded inside ingest_sgs.R as an editable default.</span>

Put the trip blank site mapping in config.yml.

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01TCA1fmfMWTVUdKSTxtq9ir"><script type="application/json">{"version":2,"name":"write","input":{"file_path":"D:\\GitHub\\kenai-river-wqx-qaqc\\functions\\ingest_sgs.R","content":"# ingest_sgs.R\n#\n# Reads one season of SGS EDD results and returns data that satisfy the `...[truncated, total 6546 chars]"},"caption":"functions/ingest_sgs.R","captionLink":"functions/ingest_sgs.R","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"# ingest_sgs.R\n#\n# Reads one season of SGS EDD results and returns data that satisfy the `dat`\n# contract (reference/dat_contract.md), plus a separate lab QC table.\n#\n# Usage:\n#   config \u003c- yaml::read_yaml(here::here(\"config.yml\"))\n#   sgs \u003c- ingest_sgs(\"spring\", config)\n#   sgs$dat      # field results (contract columns)\n#   sgs$lab_qc   # lab QC rows, incl. (surr) analytes; never goes to the WQX export\n#\n# Year-specific settings (file path, sheet, trip blank site mapping) come from\n# config.yml; nothing about a particular year is hardcoded here.\n\n# Lab QC sample-type codes (SGS EDD `sample_type`). TB (trip blank) is field data.\nsgs_lab_qc_types \u003c- c(\"MB\", \"LCS\", \"LCSD\", \"MS\", \"MSD\", \"CB\", \"OS\",\n                      \"ICV\", \"CCV\", \"LLQC\", \"QCS\", \"ICB\", \"CVC\", \"IB\")\n\ningest_sgs \u003c- function(season,\n                       config,\n                       data_dir      = here::here(\"data\"),\n                       templates_dir = here::here(\"reference/wqx_templates\")) {\n  stopifnot(season %in% c(\"spring\", \"summer\"))\n  suppressPackageStartupMessages({\n    library(dplyr); library(readxl); library(janitor)\n    library(stringr); library(lubridate); library(hms)\n  })\n\n  spec \u003c- config$files$sgs[[season]]\n  if (is.null(spec)) stop(\"config.yml has no files$sgs$\", season, call. = FALSE)\n  path \u003c- file.path(data_dir, spec$path)\n  if (!file.exists(path)) stop(\"SGS file not found: \", path, call. = FALSE)\n\n  raw \u003c- read_excel(path, sheet = spec$sheet) |>\n    clean_names() |>\n    remove_empty(which = c(\"rows\", \"cols\"))\n\n  # Dates arrive as \"MM/DD/YYYY HH:MM\" strings\n  raw \u003c- raw |>\n    mutate(\n      collect_dt   = mdy_hm(collect_date),\n      collect_date = as.Date(collect_dt),\n      collect_time = as_hms(collect_dt),\n      rec_dt       = mdy_hm(rec_date),\n      rec_date     = as.Date(rec_dt),\n      rec_time     = as_hms(rec_dt),\n      run_dt       = mdy_hm(run_date_time),\n      run_date     = as.Date(run_dt),\n      run_time     = as_hms(run_dt),\n      # EP200.8 -> 200.8\n      analytical_method = str_replace(analytical_method, \"^EP\", \"\"),\n      # Metals only; nutrients and others are filled by format_wqx.R lookups\n      result_sample_fraction = case_when(\n        dissolved == \"L\" & analytical_method == \"200.8\" ~ \"Dissolved\",\n        dissolved == \".\" & analytical_method == \"200.8\" ~ \"Unfiltered\",\n        TRUE ~ NA_character_\n      ),\n      sample_id = str_trim(sample_id)\n    ) |>\n    select(-collect_dt, -rec_dt, -run_dt, -run_date_time)\n\n  analysis_codes \u003c- read_excel(file.path(templates_dir, \"analysis_code_matching_table.xlsx\")) |>\n    clean_names() |>\n    select(sgs_analysis_code, epa_analysis_id, context_code) |>\n    mutate(sgs_analysis_code = str_replace(sgs_analysis_code, \"^EP\", \"\")) |>\n    rename(analytical_method = sgs_analysis_code)\n\n  site_names \u003c- read_excel(file.path(templates_dir, \"sgs_site_names_matching_table.xlsx\")) |>\n    clean_names() |>\n    select(sgs_sample_id, monitoring_location_id, sample_condition) |>\n    mutate(sgs_sample_id = str_trim(sgs_sample_id),\n           monitoring_location_id = as.character(monitoring_location_id)) |>\n    rename(sample_id = sgs_sample_id)\n\n  raw \u003c- raw |>\n    left_join(analysis_codes, by = \"analytical_method\") |>\n    left_join(site_names, by = \"sample_id\")\n\n  # Lab QC: the 14 QC sample types, plus surrogates from any sample type\n  is_lab_qc \u003c- raw$sample_type %in% sgs_lab_qc_types |\n    str_detect(raw$analyte, fixed(\"(surr)\", ignore_case = TRUE))\n\n  lab_qc \u003c- raw[is_lab_qc, ] |>\n    mutate(\n      lab_name = \"SGS\",\n      season   = season,\n      result   = suppressWarnings(as.numeric(result)),\n      qc_group = case_when(\n        str_detect(analyte, fixed(\"(surr)\", ignore_case = TRUE)) ~ \"surrogate\",\n        sample_type %in% c(\"LCS\", \"LCSD\", \"MS\", \"MSD\", \"QCS\")    ~ \"accuracy\",\n        sample_type %in% c(\"ICV\", \"CCV\", \"CVC\")                  ~ \"calibration\",\n        sample_type %in% c(\"MB\", \"CB\", \"ICB\", \"IB\")              ~ \"blank\",\n        TRUE                                                     ~ \"other\"\n      )\n    ) |>\n    select(sample_type, qc_group, analyte, analytical_method, result, resultflag,\n           repdl, units, amount_spiked, initial_conc, percent_recovered,\n           rec_limit_low, rec_limit_high, sample_rpd, rpd_limit_low, rpd_limit_high,\n           anl_batch, prp_batch, sdg, lab_sample_id, sample_id, collect_date,\n           run_date, dissolved, lab_name, season)\n\n  dat \u003c- raw[!is_lab_qc, ] |>\n    filter(!is.na(result)) |>\n    mutate(\n      lab_name = \"SGS\",\n      result   = as.numeric(result),\n      note     = sdg\n    ) |>\n    select(\n      sample = sample_id, lab_sample = lab_sample_id, lab_name, analyte, result,\n      units, resultflag, loq, lod, collect_date, collect_time, rec_date, rec_time,\n      run_date, run_time, analytical_method, epa_analysis_id, context_code,\n      monitoring_location_id, note, sample_type, sample_condition, dissolved,\n      result_sample_fraction\n    )\n\n  # Trip blanks cover two sites each; sites are listed in config.yml\n  dat \u003c- expand_trip_blanks(dat, config$sgs$trip_blank_sites)\n\n  # Fail loudly rather than carry NAs into the contract\n  if (anyNA(dat$monitoring_location_id)) {\n    stop(\"SGS sample IDs with no monitoring_location_id (add to sgs_site_names_matching_table.xlsx \",\n         \"or config.yml sgs$trip_blank_sites): \",\n         paste(unique(dat$sample[is.na(dat$monitoring_location_id)]), collapse = \"; \"),\n         call. = FALSE)\n  }\n  if (anyNA(dat$epa_analysis_id)) {\n    stop(\"SGS analytical methods with no match in analysis_code_matching_table.xlsx: \",\n         paste(unique(dat$analytical_method[is.na(dat$epa_analysis_id)]), collapse = \"; \"),\n         call. = FALSE)\n  }\n  if (any(grepl(\"^mg/l$\", dat$units, ignore.case = TRUE) & dat$units != \"mg/L\")) {\n    stop(\"SGS units contain a non-WQX capitalization of mg/L\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  list(dat = dat, lab_qc = lab_qc)\n}\n\n# Duplicates rows whose `sample` is a multi-site trip blank, one row per covered site.\n# `mapping` is the parsed config.yml list: sample label -> vector of location IDs.\nexpand_trip_blanks \u003c- function(dat, mapping) {\n  need \u003c- is.na(dat$monitoring_location_id)\n  if (!any(need)) return(dat)\n  if (is.null(mapping)) mapping \u003c- list()\n  lkp \u003c- tibble::tibble(\n    sample = rep(names(mapping), lengths(mapping)),\n    monitoring_location_id = as.character(unlist(mapping, use.names = FALSE))\n  )\n  dplyr::bind_rows(\n    dat[!need, ],\n    dat[need, ] |>\n      dplyr::select(-monitoring_location_id) |>\n      dplyr::left_join(lkp, by = \"sample\", relationship = \"many-to-many\")\n  )\n}\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

`functions/ingest_sgs.R` is written and gives the same SGS field data as the existing `2025.qmd` code, minus the 24 surrogate rows. I did not change `2025.qmd`, so the monolithic pipeline still runs on its own ingest code.

**What I added**
- `ingest_sgs(season, config)` returns `list(dat, lab_qc)`. The file path and sheet come from `config.yml`, and the lookup tables from `reference/wqx_templates/`. Nothing specific to 2025 is hardcoded in the function.
- `config.yml` has a new `sgs: trip_blank_sites` block mapping each trip blank label to its two site IDs. `expand_trip_blanks()` in the same file uses it.
- `lab_qc` holds the 14 QC sample types plus any `(surr)` analyte. It carries a derived `qc_group` column (accuracy, calibration, blank, surrogate, other), the recovery and RPD limits, batch IDs, `lab_name` and `season`.
- The function stops with a clear message if any sample ID has no `monitoring_location_id`, if any method has no `epa_analysis_id`, or if the `mg/L` capitalization is wrong.

**Verification** (spring plus summer)
- `dat` has 543 rows. That is the old 567 minus the 24 surrogates.
- After sorting, `all.equal` against the current `dat_sgs` returned TRUE on the 24 contract columns. In that comparison I cast the old `monitoring_location_id` to character, as the contract requires.
- `lab_qc` has 914 rows: 279 calibration, 315 blank, 199 accuracy, 85 other and 36 surrogate. The 36 surrogates are the 24 from field samples plus 12 inside QC samples.
- Units are `mg/L` (92 rows) and `ug/L` (451 rows) only.

I haven't checked that the 914 `lab_qc` rows match the code counts in `reference/dat_contract.md`.

That changes the plan, so I'll set out what changes and ask the first question. I haven't changed any files for this.

**New model:** this repo ends as a blank, year-agnostic template. For each year you copy it into a new GitHub repo and drop that year's raw files into `data/`, which is already the designated place. 2025 is the test fixture while we build.

**What this changes in the approved plan**
- **Dropped:** the "one working year, then archive" mechanics. `fetch_raw_data.R`, `archive_year.R`, the `raw-data-{year}` GitHub Releases, the `data/{year}.sha256` files, and the "backwards from 2025" sequence in Phase E are no longer needed, because each year's repo holds its own data. Edit for each data year; archived in the year bundle.\nyear: 2025\n\nsampling:\n  spring_date: \"2025-04-30\"\n  summer_date: \"2025-07-23\"\n\n# All paths are relative to data/ (raw lab files for the working year only)\nfiles:\n  sgs:\n    spring: {path: \"spring_2025_wqx_data/SGS/spring_2025_kenai_baseline_sgs_results.xlsx\", sheet: \"Sheet8\"}\n    summer: {path: \"summer_2025_wqx_data/SGS/summer_2025_kenai_baseline_results_sgs.xlsx\", sheet: \"Sheet8\"}\n  fc:\n    spring: {path: \"spring_2025_wqx_data/SWWTP/KRWF Fecal 04-30-25.xls\", skip: 11}\n    summer: {path: \"summer_2025_wqx_data/SWWTP/KRWF Fecal 07-23-25.xls\", skip: 10}\n  tss:\n    spring: {path: \"spring_2025_wqx_data/SWWTP/KRWF TSS MONITORING 05-01-25.xlsx\", sheet: \"Updated_Formatting\", skip: 0}\n    summer: {path: \"summer_2025_wqx_data/SWWTP/KRWF TSS MONITORING 07-25-25.xls\", sheet: \"Updated_Formatting\", skip: 1}\n  ysi:\n    both: {path: \"2025 Kenai Agency Baseline YSI ProQuatro and Turbidity Data.xlsx\"}\n\nsgs:\n  # Trip blanks labelled \"RMx&y trip blank\" cover two sites; one row per site is created at ingest.\n  trip_blank_sites:\n    \"RM1.5&6.5 trip blank\": [10000002, 10000005]   # Kenai City Dock, Cunningham Park\n    \"RM40&43 trip blank\":   [10000027, 10000028]   # Bing's Landing, Upstream of Dow Island\n"},"elapsedMs":93}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01TY3ktVFgBcLbYTbP12Gx2q"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 6.4KB, 156 lines, showing lines 1-80]\n1\t# ingest_sgs.R\n2\t#\n3\t# Reads one season of ...[truncated, total 3648 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 6.4KB, 156 lines, showing lines 1-80]\n# ingest_sgs.R\n#\n# Reads one season of SGS EDD results and returns data that satisfy the `dat`\n# contract (reference/dat_contract.md), plus a separate lab QC table.\n#\n# Usage:\n#   config \u003c- yaml::read_yaml(here::here(\"config.yml\"))\n#   sgs \u003c- ingest_sgs(\"spring\", config)\n#   sgs$dat      # field results (contract columns)\n#   sgs$lab_qc   # lab QC rows, incl. (surr) analytes; never goes to the WQX export\n#\n# Year-specific settings (file path, sheet, trip blank site mapping) come from\n# config.yml; nothing about a particular year is hardcoded here.\n\n# Lab QC sample-type codes (SGS EDD `sample_type`). TB (trip blank) is field data.\nsgs_lab_qc_types \u003c- c(\"MB\", \"LCS\", \"LCSD\", \"MS\", \"MSD\", \"CB\", \"OS\",\n                      \"ICV\", \"CCV\", \"LLQC\", \"QCS\", \"ICB\", \"CVC\", \"IB\")\n\ningest_sgs \u003c- function(season,\n                       config,\n                       data_dir      = here::here(\"data\"),\n                       templates_dir = here::here(\"reference/wqx_templates\")) {\n  stopifnot(season %in% c(\"spring\", \"summer\"))\n  suppressPackageStartupMessages({\n    library(dplyr); library(readxl); library(janitor)\n    library(stringr); library(lubridate); library(hms)\n  })\n\n  spec \u003c- config$files$sgs[[season]]\n  if (is.null(spec)) stop(\"config.yml has no files$sgs$\", season, call. = FALSE)\n  path \u003c- file.path(data_dir, spec$path)\n  if (!file.exists(path)) stop(\"SGS file not found: \", path, call. = FALSE)\n\n  raw \u003c- read_excel(path, sheet = spec$sheet) |>\n    clean_names() |>\n    remove_empty(which = c(\"rows\", \"cols\"))\n\n  # Dates arrive as \"MM/DD/YYYY HH:MM\" strings\n  raw \u003c- raw |>\n    mutate(\n      collect_dt   = mdy_hm(collect_date),\n      collect_date = as.Date(collect_dt),\n      collect_time = as_hms(collect_dt),\n      rec_dt       = mdy_hm(rec_date),\n      rec_date     = as.Date(rec_dt),\n      rec_time     = as_hms(rec_dt),\n      run_dt       = mdy_hm(run_date_time),\n      run_date     = as.Date(run_dt),\n      run_time     = as_hms(run_dt),\n      # EP200.8 -> 200.8\n      analytical_method = str_replace(analytical_method, \"^EP\", \"\"),\n      # Metals only; nutrients and others are filled by format_wqx.R lookups\n      result_sample_fraction = case_when(\n        dissolved == \"L\" & analytical_method == \"200.8\" ~ \"Dissolved\",\n        dissolved == \".\" & analytical_method == \"200.8\" ~ \"Unfiltered\",\n        TRUE ~ NA_character_\n      ),\n      sample_id = str_trim(sample_id)\n    ) |>\n    select(-collect_dt, -rec_dt, -run_dt, -run_date_time)\n\n  analysis_codes \u003c- read_excel(file.path(templates_dir, \"analysis_code_matching_table.xlsx\")) |>\n    clean_names() |>\n    select(sgs_analysis_code, epa_analysis_id, context_code) |>\n    mutate(sgs_analysis_code = str_replace(sgs_analysis_code, \"^EP\", \"\")) |>\n    rename(analytical_method = sgs_analysis_code)\n\n  site_names \u003c- read_excel(file.path(templates_dir, \"sgs_site_names_matching_table.xlsx\")) |>\n    clean_names() |>\n    select(sgs_sample_id, monitoring_location_id, sample_condition) |>\n    mutate(sgs_sample_id = str_trim(sgs_sample_id),\n           monitoring_location_id = as.character(monitoring_location_id)) |>\n    rename(sample_id = sgs_sample_id)\n\n  raw \u003c- raw |>\n    left_join(analysis_codes, by = \"analytical_method\") |>\n    left_join(site_names, by = \"sample_id\")\n\n  # Lab QC: the 14 QC sample types, plus surrogates from any sample type\n  is_lab_qc \u003c- raw$sample_type %in% sgs_lab_qc_types |"},"elapsedMs":85}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01Mm4RPmzetfRSkcRvUi6EZ5"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 12.9KB, 120 lines, showing lines 1-120]\n1\t# `dat` Column Contract (DRAFT, Phase A s...[truncated, total 13657 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 12.9KB, 120 lines, showing lines 1-120]\n# `dat` Column Contract (DRAFT, Phase A step 1)\r\n\r\nStatus: draft derived from `project_year/2025.qmd` Part A, Part B (`format_wqx.R`), and\r\na profile of the actual 2025 `dat` (883 rows, 22 sites, 27 analytes, three labs).\r\nNothing here is enforced in code yet. A contract-check function will be added in Phase B.\r\n\r\nReference year: the 2021 pipeline in the report repo (`chapters/appendix_a.qmd` and\r\n`functions/appendix_a_scripts/`) is the most thoroughly troubleshot year. Where 2021 and\r\n2025 disagree, 2021 behavior is the default unless there is a stated reason to differ.\r\nOther years received little troubleshooting, so their partial work is reused only where\r\nit helps.\r\n\r\n## Purpose\r\n\r\n`dat` is the seam between ingestion and everything else. Each per-lab ingest function\r\n(`ingest_sgs`, `ingest_fc`, `ingest_tss`, `ingest_ysi`) returns a data frame that\r\nsatisfies this contract. The results are row-bound into one `dat`. Everything after that\r\n(WQX formatting, QA/QC flags, CDX export, preliminary report) assumes the contract and\r\nnever touches raw lab files. Year-specific work (file names, sheet names, site-name\r\nspellings, impossible-value exclusions) ends at this boundary.\r\n\r\nOne row is one reported result for one sample, analyte, and fraction. Lab QC rows\r\n(method blanks, LCS, MS/MSD, lab duplicates, and BTEX surrogate recoveries) are NOT in\r\n`dat`. They are ingested in the same step, designated as lab QC samples, and written to a\r\nsecond table, `lab_qc_dat` (in 2021: `sgs_als_qaqc_dat.csv`), which feeds checklist\r\nQuestion 20 (lab discrepancies, matrix spikes, blanks, QC failures). `lab_qc_dat` keeps\r\nthe extra raw columns that field data does not need (`percent_recovered`,\r\n`rec_limit_low`, `rec_limit_high`, `sample_rpd`, `rpd_limit_low`, `rpd_limit_high`,\r\n`dissolved`, etc.). Its contract is still to be written.\r\n\r\n## Columns (24)\r\n\r\n\"Req\" means required by `format_wqx.R` or later steps, so the column must exist.\r\n\"NA ok\" means the value may be missing for some rows.\r\n\r\n| # | Column | Type | Req | NA ok | Meaning and rules |\r\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\r\n| 1 | `sample` | chr | yes | no | Lab/field sample label as it appears in the source file. Used for duplicate pairing and trip-blank handling. |\r\n| 2 | `lab_sample` | chr | yes | yes | Lab's own sample ID (`lab_sample_id`). NA for YSI; NA for FC in 2025. Maps to WQX Laboratory Sample ID. |\r\n| 3 | `lab_name` | chr | yes | no | One of `SGS`, `SWWTP`, `YSI` (2025). Maps to WQX Laboratory Name. New labs add a value here. |\r\n| 4 | `analyte` | chr | yes | no | Name as reported or mapped at ingest. Must match a row in `analytes_list_manual_edit.csv` (abbreviation for Activity ID) and later the WQX characteristic list. |\r\n| 5 | `result` | dbl | yes | see rule | Numeric value. NA is allowed only for a non-detect where the lab reported no number (`resultflag == \"U\"`); 2025 has 3 such TSS rows. |\r\n| 6 | `units` | chr | yes | no | WQX capitalization, e.g. `mg/L`, `ug/L`, `uS/cm`, `deg C`, `NTU`, `MPN/100ml`, `None`. See \"Known contract violations\". |\r\n| 7 | `resultflag` | chr | yes | no | `=` (detected), `U` (not detected), `J` (estimated, below LOQ). `format_wqx.R` also accepts `ND` as not detected. |\r\n| 8 | `loq` | dbl | yes | yes | Limit of quantitation, in `units`. NA for YSI and some FC/TSS. |\r\n| 9 | `lod` | dbl | yes | yes | Limit of detection / method detection level, in `units`. NA for YSI. |\r\n| 10 | `collect_date` | Date | yes | no | Sample collection date. |\r\n| 11 | `collect_time` | hms | yes | yes | Collection time. Unrecorded for most YSI rows in 2025 (177 of 222). |\r\n| 12 | `rec_date` | Date | no | yes | Lab receipt date. Used in the hold-time check (Q17). YSI sets it equal to `collect_date`. |\r\n| 13 | `rec_time` | hms | no | yes | Lab receipt time. |\r\n| 14 | `run_date` | Date | yes | no | Analysis date. Maps to WQX Analysis Start Date. |\r\n| 15 | `run_time` | hms | yes | yes | Analysis time. |\r\n| 16 | `analytical_method` | chr | yes | no | Method as the lab reports it, with any `EP` prefix removed (`200.8`, `SM21 4500NO3-F`, `2540-D`, `9222D`, `SW8260D`). `format_wqx.R` joins fraction, preservative, and container lookups on this value. |\r\n| 17 | `epa_analysis_id` | chr | yes | no | Method ID for WQX Result Analytical Method ID. Equals `analytical_method` for YSI; differs from it in 164 of 883 rows via the analysis-code matching table. |\r\n| 18 | `context_code` | chr | yes | no | WQX method context, e.g. `USEPA`, `APHA`. |\r\n| 19 | `monitoring_location_id` | chr | yes | no | Bare numeric ID, e.g. `10000002`, with NO `KENAI_WQX-` prefix (WQX Web adds it; a prefix produced the doubled-station problem in October 2026). Stored as character. Must exist in `site_coordinates`. |\r\n| 20 | `note` | chr | no | yes | Free text; becomes Activity Comment. SGS sets it to the SDG number; other labs NA. |\r\n| 21 | `sample_type` | chr | yes | no | Lab sample-type code. In `dat`: `PS` (primary sample) or `TB` (trip blank) in 2025. Lab QC codes (`MB`, `LCS`, ...) must have been removed. |\r\n| 22 | `sample_condition` | chr | yes | yes | NA for primary samples, otherwise `Field Duplicate`, `Field Blank`, or `Trip Blank`. Drives Activity ID suffix and Activity Type. |\r\n| 23 | `dissolved` | chr | no | yes | SGS's raw flag (`L` filtered, `.` unfiltered, `T`). NA for non-SGS rows. Kept in `dat` (user decision 2026-10-28) as well as in `lab_qc_dat`; `result_sample_fraction` is the working field. |\r\n| 24 | `result_sample_fraction` | chr | yes | yes | `Dissolved`, `Total`, `Unfiltered`, or NA. NA is acceptable at ingest; `format_wqx.R` fills it from `wqx_template_matching_table.xlsx` and overrides TSS (`Suspended`) and FC (`None`). A value set at ingest wins over the lookup. |\r\n\r\n## Invariants the contract check should test\r\n\r\n1. All 24 columns present with the types above (`Date`, `hms`, `dbl`, `chr`).\r\n2. `lab_name`, `analyte`, `units`, `resultflag`, `analytical_method`, `epa_analysis_id`, `context_code`, `monitoring_location_id`, `sample_type`, `collect_date`, `run_date`, `sample` have no NA.\r\n3. `resultflag %in% c(\"=\", \"U\", \"J\", \"ND\")`. `result` is non-NA unless `resultflag` is `U` or `ND`.\r\n4. `units` uses WQX capitalization; no lowercase `mg/l`.\r\n5. `monitoring_location_id` matches `^[0-9]+$` (no prefix) and is present in `site_coordinates`.\r\n6. No lab QC sample types (`MB`, `LCS`, `LCSD`, `MS`, `MSD`, `CB`, `OS`, `ICV`, `CCV`, `LLQC`, `QCS`, `ICB`, `CVC`, `IB`).\r\n7. `sample_condition` is NA or in `{Field Duplicate, Field Blank, Trip Blank}`.\r\n8. `analyte` is in the analyte abbreviation list; `analytical_method` is in the fraction, preservative, and container lookups (unmapped values should stop the run, not silently produce NA).\r\n9. No duplicated key of (`monitoring_location_id`, `collect_date`, `analyte`, `result_sample_fraction`, `sample_condition`, `epa_analysis_id`). Activity ID uniqueness is what the WQX import depends on.\r\n\r\n## Known contract violations in the current 2025 `dat`\r\n\r\nFound by running Part A of `2025.qmd` in a separate R process and profiling the result.\r\n\r\n1. **`units` mixes `mg/l` and `mg/L`.** All 48 `mg/l` rows are SWWTP total suspended solids. Part A of `2025.qmd` writes `\"mg/l\"` literally in both the spring and summer TSS chunks. The report repo fixed the same bug in its own `ingest_tss.R` on 2026-10-20; the qaqc repo copy still has it. Whether `format_wqx.R` or the export fixes it later is unverified.\r\n2. **`matrix` is `\"1\"` for all 567 SGS rows** and `\"Water\"` for FC, TSS, and YSI. The SGS EDD column carries a numeric code. `format_wqx.R` does not read `matrix`, so nothing breaks, so the column is dropped from `dat` (resolved 2026-10-28).\r\n3. **BTEX surrogate recoveries leaked into `dat` and into the WQX file.** Three analytes (`1,2-Dichloroethane-D4 (surr)`, `4-Bromofluorobenzene (surr)`, `Toluene-d8 (surr)`; units `%`; 24 rows) pass through Part A of `2025.qmd` because its `lab_qc_types` list has no surrogate code, and all 24 appear in `2025_kwf_baseline_results_wqx.csv` as `Field Msr/Obs`. The 2021 pipeline handled these correctly: `ingest_sgs_als.R` routes any analyte containing `(surr)` to `sgs_als_qaqc_dat.csv` and removes it from the field data. **Decision (user, 2026-10-28):** keep surrogates in the ingest as designated lab QC samples, in `lab_qc_dat`, not in `dat`. The template should also evaluate them under Question 20 (the 2021 chapter reviews matrix spikes only).\r\n4. **`result_detection_condition` is set at ingest and then overwritten** by `format_wqx.R`. Dropped from `dat` (resolved 2026-10-28).\r\n5. **Spring and summer ingest code is duplicated** (SGS, FC, TSS each have two near-identical chunks), which is part of why per-lab functions taking a season argument are the Phase B target.\r\n\r\n## Open questions\r\n\r\n- RESOLVED 2026-10-28: `matrix` and `result_detection_condition` are dropped from `dat` (constant and recomputed by `format_wqx.R`, respectively). `dissolved` stays in both `dat` and `lab_qc_dat`.\r\n- RESOLVED 2026-10-28: field blank coding, see \"Field blank handling\" below.\r\n- Is `sample_type` needed beyond `PS`/`TB`? Field duplicates are already carried by `sample_condition`; `format_wqx.R` also tests `sample_condition == \"Blank\"`, which no ingest step produces (it emits `Trip Blank` and `Field Blank`), so that branch looks dead and its effect on Activity Type for blanks should be confirmed.\r\n- Should the assessed output (`assessed_results.csv`, Phase C) use this schema plus flag columns, or the WQX-formatted names? Not decided; to be settled when Phase C is scoped.\r\n\r\n## Field blank handling (decided 2026-10-28)\r\n\r\n`Field Blank` is a `sample_condition` value in 2025 (44 `dat` rows; the 2022 QAPP change introduced a dissolved metals field blank). Years before about late 2022 have none, so field-blank handling is optional per year.\r\n\r\n**Decision (user, 2026-10-28):**\r\n- **Activity Type:** `Quality Control Sample-Field Blank` (QFB), the code in the WQX ActivityType domain list. It requires an analytical method; no monitoring location constraint.\r\n- **Activity ID suffix:** `FB`. The existing trip blank suffix `Blank` is unchanged, so Activity IDs already on WQP do not change. A dissolved and a total field blank for the same analyte, site, and date share one Activity ID; `Result Sample Fraction` distinguishes them.\r\n- **How's My Waterway:** HMW's measurement charts drop any record whose Activity Type contains \"quality\" (`MonitoringReport.tsx`), so this code keeps field blanks out of HMW charts. Station summary counts and WQP downloads are not filtered.\r\n\r\n**Current defect in `format_wqx.R` (2025):** the suffix and Activity Type logic knows only `Field Duplicate` and `Trip Blank`. In the 2025 intermediate export, 56 Activity IDs end in `-NA` (for example `10000002-2025-04-30-Cd-NA`), consistent with field blanks getting no suffix (44 field blanks; the extra 12 rows are not yet explained). All 883 rows are `Field Msr/Obs` (767), field replicate (80), or trip blank (36), so field blanks would upload as ordinary field results. Fix in the template before any 2025 upload, and add a contract invariant: every `Field Blank` row maps to the QFB Activity Type and an `FB` suffix.\r\n\r\n## `lab_qc_dat` (profiled from 2025 SGS EDDs, 2026-10-28)\r\n\r\nBoth 2025 SGS files (Sheet8) share the same 42 columns. `sample_type` values: PS (primary sample), TB (trip blank, summer only, 18 rows; field data, not lab QC), and 14 lab QC codes. Counts are spring/summer combined.\r\n\r\n| Group | Codes (rows) | Limits populated |\r\n|---|---|---|\r\n| Accuracy (spike recovery) | LCS (72), LCSD (15), MS (65), MSD (14), QCS (39) | `percent_recovered`, `rec_limit_low/high` always; `sample_rpd`, `rpd_limit_low/high` for LCSD (RPD limits 80%) and MSD |\r\n| Calibration checks | ICV (39), CCV (237), CVC (9) | recovery and limits always |\r\n| Blanks | MB (72), CB (227), ICB (13), IB (9) | no recovery or limits; result vs `repdl`/LOQ is the check (a few MB/IB rows carry recovery, apparently surrogates) |\r\n| Other | LLQC (45), OS (40) | LLQC: recovery but no limits; OS: no recovery or limits |\r\n\r\nFindings:\r\n- **Limits ship with the EDD.** Recovery and RPD limits are columns, so Question 20 can use the lab's own limits and no hardcoded thresholds are needed (except LLQC and OS, which have none).\r\n- **Surrogates (`(surr)` analytes, 24 rows in PS, plus rows within QC codes such as CCV, CVC, IB, LCS)** appear in both field samples and QC samples. Rule: route any `(surr)` analyte to `lab_qc_dat` regardless of `sample_type`.\r\n- **`lab_qc_types` in `2025.qmd` is the right list** (MB, LCS, LCSD, MS, MSD, CB, OS, ICV, CCV, LLQC, QCS, ICB, CVC, IB), but it omits the surrogate rule. TB is correctly kept out.\r\n- **Proposed `lab_qc_dat` columns:** `sample_type`, `qc_group` (accuracy, calibration, blank, other; derived), `analyte`, `analytical_method`, `result`, `resultflag`, `repdl`, `units`, `amount_spiked`, `initial_conc`, `percent_recovered`, `rec_limit_low/high`, `sample_rpd`, `rpd_limit_low/high`, `anl_batch`, `prp_batch`, `sdg`, `lab_sample_id`, `sample_id`, `collect_date`, `run_date`, `dissolved`, `lab_name`, `season`. Spring/summer file differences: none found.\r\n- **Not yet checked:** whether the 2021 ALS layout carries the same limit columns; this is the first adapter test.\r\n"},"elapsedMs":94}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><thinking id="thinking-1793229484760-wa9jg">
I'm spotting inconsistencies between the 2025 script and the report repo: differing units and method names for fecal coliform, a hardcoded run_date, placeholder rec_time, and blanks/positives being dropped instead of routed to lab_qc, while the contract and 2021 reference use different unit conventions altogether.

I'm also thinking the function shouldn't hardcode site lookups—better to pull from a shared reference table, though mapping "RM x" labels to site IDs needs more investigation. Rather than dig deeper now, I'll surface the design with open decisions one at a time, starting with config structure: replacing hardcoded skip-row counts with auto-detection of the header row, and auto-detecting time format based on value ranges instead of hardcoding it per season.

For run dates, I'll require them from config rather than hardcoding, and use NA rather than placeholder zeros for missing receipt date/time. Blanks and positives (MB, LCS) should route to lab_qc like the sgs pipeline does, returning a list of the main data plus lab_qc with a compatible column subset, while N/A results get dropped and reported in the summary message. I'll keep my response concise—present the overall design, then ask about just the first decision (FC units: cfu/100mL vs MPN/100mL), noting more decisions will follow afterward.


</thinking><span data-thinking-duration-ms="10490"></span></div>

I read the report repo's `ingest_fc.R`, the two FC chunks in `2025.qmd`, `config.yml` and the contract. Nothing is written yet.

**How the two existing versions differ**
- **Report repo (2021):** It has per-lab parser blocks (SWWTP, Taurianen). Lab blanks and positive controls go to a QC file. Units are `cfu/100ml`, the method is the long "9222 D ~ ..." string, and `loq` is 1.
- **`2025.qmd`:** Both seasons are inline, near-duplicate chunks. They drop blanks and positives instead of keeping them. Units are `MPN/100ml`, the method is `9222D`, and receipt time is a placeholder `00:00`.
- **Hardcoded values in `2025.qmd`:** The site lookups are embedded in the chunks, and the run dates (5/1/2025 and 7/24/2025) are written in directly.

**Proposed `ingest_fc(season, config, ...)`**
Like `ingest_sgs()`, it would return `list(dat, lab_qc)`. Nothing year-specific lives in the function; the 2025 specifics go in `config.yml` or reference tables.

| Concern | 2025 chunks | Template design |
|---|---|---|
| Header row | `skip = 11` / `skip = 10` | Find the row containing "Dish Number" and read from there. No `skip` in config. |
| Time Sampled format | Spring Excel fraction, summer HHMM integer | Detect per file: values below 1 are Excel fractions, values of 100 or more are HHMM. Stop on anything else. |
| Site names | Inline tribbles per season | Parse the label (strip `DUP`, `RM `) and look it up in one shared table, so DUP sites need no per-season lists. Stop on unmatched labels. |
| Blanks and positives | Dropped | Go to `lab_qc` as MB and LCS, with `qc_group = "blank"` / `"accuracy"`. |
| Results of `N/A` | Dropped silently | Dropped with a message listing the sites. |
| Run date, receipt date and time | Hardcoded or `00:00` | Run date required in `config.yml` (stop if missing). Receipt date and time optional in config, otherwise NA. |
| `result_detection_condition`, `matrix` | Set at ingest | Dropped, per the contract. |
| Units, method, LOQ | See decision 1 | See decision 1 |

Taurianen (the old summer FC lab) is not needed for any year after 2021. I'd leave it out and add a clear "unknown lab format" stop, so a different lab layout fails loudly.

**Decision 1: FC units and method**
The 2021 reference uses `cfu/100ml`, method `9222 D ~ Membrane filtration...` and LOQ 1. `2025.qmd` uses `MPN/100ml`, `9222D` and no LOQ. Membrane filtration produces colony counts, so `cfu/100ml` is the correct unit for method 9222D. `MPN` belongs to multiple-tube methods. I'd use `cfu/100ml` with `analytical_method = "9222D"`, `epa_analysis_id = "9222D"` and `loq = 1` (from the QAPP). I'd also check that the WQX unit list accepts `cfu/100ml` and that `format_wqx.R` doesn't depend on `MPN`.

Do you want `cfu/100ml`, or should I keep `MPN/100ml` for continuity with what's already on WQP?

After this I'll ask about the site lookup table, the run-date config shape and the `lab_qc` columns, one at a time.


<span class="suggestion">Use cfu/100ml for fecal coliform, check the WQX unit list, then continue with the site lookup decision.</span>


<span class="suggestion">Keep MPN/100ml for continuity with WQP and move on to the site lookup decision.</span>

**Decision 2: site lookup**

`reference/baseline_sites.csv` already has `river_mile` and `tributary_mainstem` for all 22 sites, with the ID in `MonitoringLocationIdentifier` (including the `KENAI_WQX-` prefix, which I'd strip). The river-mile values are unique across the 22 sites (for example, tributary 10 and mainstem 10.1 do not collide). So I can resolve a lab label to a site by parsing the number, with no new lookup table:

- `RM 22 DUP` becomes river mile 22, which is site `10000022`, with `sample_condition` = Field Duplicate.
- `RM O`, `RM22` and `RM_10_DUP` (the TSS quirks from 2025) normalize through the same rule.
- Any label that doesn't resolve stops the run and prints the label.

One shared helper, `resolve_rm_site(labels)`, would serve `ingest_fc`, `ingest_tss` and `ingest_ysi`. It would be generic rather than a per-lab lookup.

The alternative is a per-lab matching table, like the `sgs_site_names_matching_table.xlsx` approach. That needs a manual edit each year when a lab renames a site, but it has no parsing rule that could misread a label.

I recommend the helper, since it removes the per-season inline tribbles. It does depend on `baseline_sites.csv` staying accurate, which it needs to anyway.

Do you want the shared river-mile helper, or per-lab matching tables?

Noted that duplicate, field blank and trip blank locations will almost always differ by year and season. That shapes the helper design:

- **`resolve_rm_site()`:** It reads the condition from the label text (`DUP`, `FIELD BLANK`, `TRIP BLANK`, and for FC `BLANK` or `POSITIVE`). It never uses a list of sites that have duplicates. It returns `monitoring_location_id` and `sample_condition` for each label. A new duplicate site needs no code or config change.
- **Unresolved labels:** A label it can't parse stops the run and prints the label, so the failure is visible.
- **SGS:** This is the one place that still needs a per-year edit. `sgs_site_names_matching_table.xlsx` is keyed on the lab's sample ID strings, so each year's new duplicate or blank IDs need rows added. `ingest_sgs()` already stops on an NA location ID. Likewise, `trip_blank_sites` in `config.yml` is edited each year, since trip blank labels cover different site pairs. Both go on the README's new-year checklist.

**Decision 3: where FC run dates come from**

I read the header block of both 2025 SWWTP FC files. Each has `DATE IN: MM-DD-YY` and `DATE OUT: MM-DD-YY` above the data table:

| Season | DATE IN | DATE OUT |
|---|---|---|
| Spring | 04-30-25 | 05-01-25 |
| Summer | 07-23-25 | 07-24-25 |

DATE OUT matches the dates hardcoded in `2025.qmd` (5/1 and 7/24). The "verify against the SWWTP lab report" note on the summer date is therefore answered by the file itself. Each data row also has `Time In` and `Time Out`, as Excel fractions in spring and HHMM in summer.

I propose reading the run date and time from the file, so no run-date config is needed. If the `DATE IN` line is missing, the function stops. The mapping would be:

- **`run_date` and `run_time`:** DATE IN plus the row's `Time In`. This is the analysis start, and the contract says `run_date` maps to WQX Analysis Start Date.
- **Analysis end:** DATE OUT plus `Time Out` would go in the `note`. It isn't a contract column.

This changes the 2025 spring `run_date` from 5/1 to 4/30, and summer from 7/24 to 7/23. The old chunks used the end date with a `00:00` time. The start date is also what the Q17 hold-time check needs for FC. Receipt date and time aren't in the file, so that is the next decision.

For FC specifically, I should have run_date default to collect_date and run_time come from Time In, with a cross-check against the header DATE IN to warn on mismatches. For the receipt decision, I'm weighing two options: leave rec_date/rec_time as NA for FC and have Q17 fall back to analysis start time when receipt is missing, versus setting rec equal to run start which keeps Q17 unchanged but misrepresents when the lab actually received the sample. I'm leaning toward the first approach — leave receipt NA and let Q17 use analysis start for holding-time methods like FC where that's the defined endpoint — but I want to verify the actual holding time limit for method 9222D in the holding times reference file before finalizing this.

Understood: the FC run date equals the collection date. So `run_date` comes from the collection date in `config.yml`, and `run_time` comes from each row's `Time In`. I'll also cross-check the file's `DATE IN` line against the config collection date and stop if they differ. That gives a guard without a separate run-date setting.

**Decision 4: receipt date and time for FC**

`2025.qmd` sets FC `rec_date` to the collection date and `rec_time` to `00:00`, which is a placeholder. Q17 (hold times) uses those values. Q17 computes hold time as receipt minus collection. For FC, receipt midnight on the collection day is before the afternoon collection time, so the result is a negative number that always passes. The 6-hour FC limit in `sample_holding_times.csv` is therefore never really tested.

For FC the 6-hour limit runs from collection to the start of analysis, and the file has that clock directly (`Time In`). Receipt isn't recorded in the file at all.

I'd leave `rec_date` and `rec_time` as NA for FC at ingest. Q17 would then measure from collection to receipt where a receipt time exists, and to `run_date` plus `run_time` where it doesn't. Q17's current logic, which turns a missing receipt time into midnight, would be replaced. Otherwise NA receipts would silently pass. In the contract, `rec_*` stay "no" required and "NA ok". The same Q17 rule would apply to TSS and YSI, which also use placeholders (`rec_time` of `00:00` for TSS, `rec_time` equal to `collect_time` for YSI).

The alternative is to set FC `rec_date` and `rec_time` to the analysis start. Q17 then works unchanged, but the receipt columns would record something that didn't happen.

Understood on both points.

- **YSI:** These are in situ readings, so no sample went to a lab. `rec_date` and `rec_time` should be NA for YSI. The current chunk copies the collection time into `rec_time`, which is wrong. Hold times don't apply to them.
- **FC receipt:** The receipt date and time live only in the chain of custody (COC), which isn't in this repo. So they are an input the user supplies each season, not something the code can read or infer.

That settles the design. FC receipt goes in `config.yml` as a user-supplied value, and the README checklist tells the user to copy it from the COC:

```yaml
fc:
  spring: {path: "...", rec_date: "2025-04-30", rec_time: "13:31"}   # from chain of custody
  summer: {path: "...", rec_date: "2025-07-23", rec_time: "13:37"}
```

The dates and times above are only an illustration of the format. I don't have the 2025 COC. The old `13:31` and `13:37` values in the report repo scripts are 2021 values, and the old chunks use placeholders, so I won't carry any of them into the 2025 config. Do you have the 2025 COC, or should the 2025 fixture leave receipt blank for now?

Q17 also needs two fixes later, in Phase C:

- **Missing receipt:** it currently turns a missing `rec_time` into midnight.
- **Missing hold time:** it marks a row `Y` (pass) when no holding time exists for the method, and that includes YSI. Those rows should be `not applicable` instead, so a gap can't pass silently.

**Decision 5: if a season's FC receipt is missing from config**

- **Option A (my recommendation):** `ingest_fc()` warns and sets `rec_date` and `rec_time` to NA. Q17 then lists those FC rows as "not evaluable". The pipeline runs, and the QA/QC document shows the gap.
- **Option B:** `ingest_fc()` stops with a message pointing to the COC. This forces the user to supply the values before anything runs, but the template can't run a first pass on the data without them.

I lean towards A, because the missing value still shows up in the QA/QC output and doesn't stop all the other checks.

Recorded. These are the settled design points for `ingest_fc()` and `resolve_rm_site()`. Nothing is written yet.

**`resolve_rm_site(labels)`** (shared by FC, TSS and YSI)
- It takes the lab's site labels and parses the river-mile number. It matches that to `river_mile` in `reference/baseline_sites.csv`, strips the `KENAI_WQX-` prefix, and returns a bare `monitoring_location_id`.
- It reads `sample_condition` from the label text (`DUP` gives Field Duplicate, and so on). No lists of duplicate or blank sites are kept.
- It handles the TSS spellings (`RM O`, `RM22`, `RM_10_DUP`).
- It stops and prints any label it can't resolve.

**`ingest_fc(season, config, ...)`** returns `list(dat, lab_qc)`
- **File:** path from `config.yml`. The header row is found by searching for "Dish Number", so there is no `skip` setting.
- **Time Sampled and Time In:** the format (Excel fraction or HHMM) is detected per column. Anything else stops the run.
- **`collect_date`:** from `sampling.{season}_date` in config.
- **`run_date`:** the same as `collect_date`. `run_time` comes from each row's `Time In`. The function checks the file's `DATE IN` line against the config date and stops if they differ.
- **Receipt:** `rec_date` and `rec_time` come from the season's entry in `config.yml` (from the COC). If they're missing, it warns and sets both to NA.
- **Units and method:** `cfu/100mL`, `9222D`, `epa_analysis_id` of `9222D`, `context_code` of `APHA`, `loq` of 1, `lab_name` of `SWWTP`.
- **Result handling:** `N/A` rows are dropped with a message listing the sites. Zero counts give `result` NA and `resultflag` "U". Other counts give "=".
- **Lab QC:** rows labelled BLANK or POSITIVE go to `lab_qc` (MB and LCS). They carry the same column names as the SGS `lab_qc` where applicable, with NA for columns FC doesn't have (recovery limits, for instance).
- **Dropped columns:** `matrix` and `result_detection_condition`.

**Config and Q17 follow-ups**
- Config files get FC `rec_date` and `rec_time` fields. The 2025 values stay blank until you have the COC.
- Q17 reports NA receipt as "not evaluable" and YSI as "not applicable". I'll do this in Phase C, not now.
- The contract's `MPN/100ml` example changes to `cfu/100mL`, and `units` gets the corrected FC value.

**Verification plan:** after writing the function, I'll compare `dat` with the existing `dat_fc` from `2025.qmd`. I expect these differences, all by design: units, `run_date` and `run_time`, `rec_date` and `rec_time` now NA, and the dropped columns. The results, sites and conditions should match.

Lab QC: the 14 QC sample types, ...[truncated, total 3643 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 6.4KB, 156 lines, showing lines 78-156]\n\n  # Lab QC: the 14 QC sample types, plus surrogates from any sample type\n  is_lab_qc \u003c- raw$sample_type %in% sgs_lab_qc_types |\n    str_detect(raw$analyte, fixed(\"(surr)\", ignore_case = TRUE))\n\n  lab_qc \u003c- raw[is_lab_qc, ] |>\n    mutate(\n      lab_name = \"SGS\",\n      season   = season,\n      result   = suppressWarnings(as.numeric(result)),\n      qc_group = case_when(\n        str_detect(analyte, fixed(\"(surr)\", ignore_case = TRUE)) ~ \"surrogate\",\n        sample_type %in% c(\"LCS\", \"LCSD\", \"MS\", \"MSD\", \"QCS\")    ~ \"accuracy\",\n        sample_type %in% c(\"ICV\", \"CCV\", \"CVC\")                  ~ \"calibration\",\n        sample_type %in% c(\"MB\", \"CB\", \"ICB\", \"IB\")              ~ \"blank\",\n        TRUE                                                     ~ \"other\"\n      )\n    ) |>\n    select(sample_type, qc_group, analyte, analytical_method, result, resultflag,\n           repdl, units, amount_spiked, initial_conc, percent_recovered,\n           rec_limit_low, rec_limit_high, sample_rpd, rpd_limit_low, rpd_limit_high,\n           anl_batch, prp_batch, sdg, lab_sample_id, sample_id, collect_date,\n           run_date, dissolved, lab_name, season)\n\n  dat \u003c- raw[!is_lab_qc, ] |>\n    filter(!is.na(result)) |>\n    mutate(\n      lab_name = \"SGS\",\n      result   = as.numeric(result),\n      note     = sdg\n    ) |>\n    select(\n      sample = sample_id, lab_sample = lab_sample_id, lab_name, analyte, result,\n      units, resultflag, loq, lod, collect_date, collect_time, rec_date, rec_time,\n      run_date, run_time, analytical_method, epa_analysis_id, context_code,\n      monitoring_location_id, note, sample_type, sample_condition, dissolved,\n      result_sample_fraction\n    )\n\n  # Trip blanks cover two sites each; sites are listed in config.yml\n  dat \u003c- expand_trip_blanks(dat, config$sgs$trip_blank_sites)\n\n  # Fail loudly rather than carry NAs into the contract\n  if (anyNA(dat$monitoring_location_id)) {\n    stop(\"SGS sample IDs with no monitoring_location_id (add to sgs_site_names_matching_table.xlsx \",\n         \"or config.yml sgs$trip_blank_sites): \",\n         paste(unique(dat$sample[is.na(dat$monitoring_location_id)]), collapse = \"; \"),\n         call. = FALSE)\n  }\n  if (anyNA(dat$epa_analysis_id)) {\n    stop(\"SGS analytical methods with no match in analysis_code_matching_table.xlsx: \",\n         paste(unique(dat$analytical_method[is.na(dat$epa_analysis_id)]), collapse = \"; \"),\n         call. = FALSE)\n  }\n  if (any(grepl(\"^mg/l$\", dat$units, ignore.case = TRUE) & dat$units != \"mg/L\")) {\n    stop(\"SGS units contain a non-WQX capitalization of mg/L\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  list(dat = dat, lab_qc = lab_qc)\n}\n\n# Duplicates rows whose `sample` is a multi-site trip blank, one row per covered site.\n# `mapping` is the parsed config.yml list: sample label -> vector of location IDs.\nexpand_trip_blanks \u003c- function(dat, mapping) {\n  need \u003c- is.na(dat$monitoring_location_id)\n  if (!any(need)) return(dat)\n  if (is.null(mapping)) mapping \u003c- list()\n  lkp \u003c- tibble::tibble(\n    sample = rep(names(mapping), lengths(mapping)),\n    monitoring_location_id = as.character(unlist(mapping, use.names = FALSE))\n  )\n  dplyr::bind_rows(\n    dat[!need, ],\n    dat[need, ] |>\n      dplyr::select(-monitoring_location_id) |>\n      dplyr::left_join(lkp, by = \"sample\", relationship = \"many-to-many\")\n  )\n}\n"},"elapsedMs":50}</script></tool-result></div>
